S.O. 624.—In exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-clause (xvli) of Clause (c) of section 2 of the Industrial 
Development Bank of India Act, 1964 (18 of 1964), the 
Central Government hereby specifics the activity of flori¬ 
culture to be an ‘‘industrial concern'' for the purposes 
of the said sub-clause. 

Explanation.—"Floriculture” includes the cultivation, 
treatment and packaging of flowers or any other 
process connected with flowers which result in a 
value added activity.

MINISTRY OF CIVIL SUPPLIES, CONSUMER

New Delhi, the 10th Match, 1993 

S.O, C26.—Whereas the Central Government alter 
considering the report submitted to it bv the p> escribed 
authority fl.e. the Director! is satisfied that the Model des¬ 
cribed in the said report is in conformity with the provisions 
of the Standards of Weights and Measures Act, 1976 (60 


of 1976) and the Standards of Weights and Measure* 
(.Approval of Model) Rules, 1987 and the suid model is 
likely to maintain accuracy over periods of sustained use 
and to render accurate service under varied conditions; 

Now, therefore, in exctcisc of the powers conferred by 
wih-scciion 17) oi section 36 oi die su.d act, the Central 
Government hereby publish the ’approval of the model of 
the. self-indicating non-automatic weighing livdiument of type 
No 33i9 and with the brand name ‘AVERY 1 (hereinafter 
referred to as the model) manufactured by Mis. Aveiy India 
Limited, Plot-50-59, Sector 25, Ballabgarh, Haryana-121004 
and is assigned the approval mork-lND|01|92 02. 

The model is a medium accuracy (Accuracy class III) 
weighing instrument with a maximum capacity of 30 kilo¬ 
gram and a minimum capacity of 200 gram. The verifica¬ 
tion interval (c) is 10 gram (see figure 1). It has a tare 



Figure I 

device. Tho tare effect may be either 100 per cent sub- 
slractive, retained tare with manual cancellation or 100 per 
cent substraclive accumulated tare with self cancellation or 
100 per cent sutntractive retained tare with manual cancella¬ 
tion with gross weight display facility or 100 per cent sub- 
straclivc and accumulated tare with negative display or tare 
weigth. The base and the platform are made up of alumi¬ 
nium casting, The stainless steel load receptor has the 
dimension 350 millimeter inlo 3*0 milOmcter The loadicell 
is mounted between the base and the platform. The seven 
segment vacuum fluorescent display of character size 12 
imillimeter indicates the weighing result. The instrument 
operates on 220 volt, 50 Hartz alternative current power 
supply. An additional printed circuit board may also be 
provided so that the instrument can be operated with a 12 
volt direct current battery or with both alternative current 
mains and battery. This model approval shall also cover 
the weighing instrument of type No, 3359 with u maximum 
capacity of 15 kilogram and verification scale interval 5 
gram. 

The model was put to accuracy test, time dependent tests, 
eccentricity test, repeatability test, discrimination test, en¬ 
durance te’st, volage and frequency variation test, electrical 
disturbance tests and static temperature test and its perfor¬ 
mance was found to be satistacun v. The instrument shall 
be ’sealed in such a manner so that no Access to parts which] 
affect the metrological characteristics shall be possible with¬ 
out damage to verification marks put on the -eal. 

This certificate of approval of model is subiect lo the 
conditions laid down in rules 14 artfi 15 of the Standards 
of Weights and Measures (Approval of Models) Ruler. 19E7. 
Further this certificate relates to the suitability of the 
equipment for use in any transaction in respect of the metro-

New Delhi, the 11th March, 195>3 
(ARCHAEOLOGY) 

SO. 632—Whereas by a notification of the Government 
of India in the Department of Culture (Archaeological Survey 
of India), No, S.O. 2242 dated 13th August, 1991 published 
in Part-Jl, reel,'on 3, sub-section (ii) of the Gazette of India 
dated the 24th August 1991, the Central Government gave 
two months notice of it intention to declare the St. Paul's 
Chinch at Diu, specified in the Schedule to the said notifica¬ 
tion, to be of national importance, and a copy of the said 
notification was affixed at a conspicuous place near the said 
Church as required by sub-section (1) of section 4 of the 


Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Ramains 
Act, 1958 (24 of 1938), 

And whereas the said Gazette was made avadable to 
the public on the 16th September, 1991; 

And whereas no objection from the public has been received 
by the Central Government within the said period of two 
months; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-soction (3) of section 4 of the Ancient Monuments and 
Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 1958, the Central 
Government hereby declares the St. Paul’s Church, specified 
In the Schedule annexed hereto, to be of national importance.

ansport of Petroleum Gas from Bhuvanagiri Well No. 2 to

; laid by the Gas Authority of Tndia Ltd.

iprline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
nd described in the schedule annexed hereto.

New Delhi, the 10th March. 1993 

SO f 37.-—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the Public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum Gas from Kuthalam (Group Gather¬ 
ing Station, Narimanam) hi Tamil Nadu State to Thirunallar 
(Spic Tine Chemicals Ltd.) in Pondicherry State detomed por¬ 
tion) Pipeline should be laid by the Gas Authority of India 
Ltd,, 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
Pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed herein. 


Now, therefore, in exorcise of the powers conferred bv 
Snlv^eclio'ii (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine- 
;/s Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in the land) Act, 
I'.'62 intention to acquire the right of user therein. 

Piovided that any person interested in the said land may, 
v.’il.hir. 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the hiy.ng of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Gas Authority of India Ltd., Cnuvery Project, 
Napunatfinam (Pin-611001); 

And everv person trialing such as objection shall also state 
spccfically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal piactitioner.

ment that it is necessary in the Public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum and Natural Gas of HBJ pipeline 
project a pipeline should be laid by the Gas Authority of 
India Limited. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
Pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine¬ 


rals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in the land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of used therein ; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Gas Authority of India Ltd., HBJ Project, Vikas 
Deep Budding, 22-Station Road, Lucknow-226019, U.P, 

And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically where he wishes to be hear in person or bv legal 
practitioner.

New Delhi, the 10th March, 1993 

S.O. 639.—Whereas by notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Petroleum S.O. 2072 dated 3rd 
August, 1992 under sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petro¬ 
leum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User 
in Land Act, 1962 (JO of 1962). the Central Government de¬ 
clared its intention to acquire the right of user in the lands 
specified in the schedule appended to that notification for 
purpose of laying pipeline; 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub-sec¬ 
tion (1) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to the 
Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the 1 right of 


user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the said 
lauds specified in the schedule appended to this notification 
hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further m exercise of power conferred by snb-section 
(4) of the section, the Central Government directs that the 
right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in Cen¬ 
tral Government vests on this date of the publication of this 
declaration the Gas Authority of India Limited free from 
encumbrances.

S.O. 648.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Natural Gas from Village-Borin, Tahasil-Alibag. 
District-Raigad to Village-Salav, Tahasil-Murud Janiirn, Dis- 
irict-Raigad, in the State of Maharashtra pipe fine should lie 
laid through the Agency of Gas Authority of India Ltd. 
16-DhiKaji Cama Place, R. K. Puram, Ring Road, New 
Delhi 110066. 

And whereas, it appears to the Central Government that 
for the' purpose of laying such pipe lines, it is necessary to 
acquire the Right of User in the lands described in the sche¬ 
dule annexed hereto; 

Novv, therefore, in exercise of the poweis conferred by 
Sub-w-'tion (I) of Section 3 of the Petroleum & Minerals 


pipe lines, (Acquisition of Right of User in the lands) Act, 
19<>2 (50 of 1962) the Central Government declares its inten¬ 
tion to acquire the Right of User in the lands referred in the 
schedule. 

Provided that any person interested in the said lands hav¬ 
ing any objection for laying the pipe lines through the said 
lands may prefer any objection within 21 days from th* 
date of notification to the Competent Authority, Thal-Salav 
Natural Gas pipe line, Prabhu Niwa3 , 2nd Floor, Alibag. 
At. post. Tahasil-Alibag, District-Raigad, State Maharashtra.

specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by a 
legal practitioner.

AWARD 

An industrial dispute between (he above-named parties has 
been referred for adjudication under section 10(1) of the 

I.D. Act, 1947 to the Industrial Tribunal, Ahmedabad by 
the Desk Officer, Labour Department, Government of India's 
Order No. L-12012-292-91-1R-B. 3 dated 13-12-91, Subse¬ 
quently it has been sent to this Tribunal for proper adjudi¬ 
cation. The industrial dispute relates to the question whe¬ 
ther the action of the management of Itanaskantha Mehsana 
Gramin Bank, Patan (Gujarat) in terminating the services 
of SubcAhan M. Pathan as peon w.e.f. 3-10-89 is legal and 
justified ? If not, to what relief the concerned workman is 
entitled to? 

2. The second party has in its statement of claim at Ex. 3 
contended that he was inducted in service with eilect from 
3rd October, 1984 by the Manager of the first party. lie 
continuously worked fill 3rd October. 1989. Thus he worked 
for about 5 years. The first parlv thereafter without assign¬ 
ing any reason terminated his service. He was not pil'd any 
notice pav. He wan not paid anv retrenchment compensation 
as contemplated under section 25F of (he ID. Ac*. 1947, 
Thus his services were terminated in vi'-lation of th? manda¬ 
tory provisions contained in Section 25E of the LD. Ac', 
1947, Therefore it is void-abinitio. He therefore entitled to 
reinstatement with hack wages. Ther-lorc, it has prayed to 
direct the first party to treat him as in service and to pay to 
him full back wages. 

3. The first party has resisted the statement of claim of 
the second party by filing its written statement Ex. 4, where¬ 
in it has inter alia contended that Shri Subekhan M. Pathan, 
was not posted at Virampur brnneh. He was never appoin¬ 
ted in bank’s sendee and so the question of termination of 
service does not arise Consequently there does not arise 
any question of his having served for 5 vrs. at Virampur 
branch, d he bank has appointed a messenger at its Virampur 
branch in terms of Govt, of India’s circular dated 8-10-84 
find since Shri Pathan could nof fulfill the norms laid down 
in the said circular and his name was not recommended or 
routed through employment exchange his services could not 
be regularised by the bank, Therefore it has not committed 
629 GI N 3 —in 


.* The concerned workman is examined at Ex. 7. His 
deposition is very very short. He has said that he used to 
work from 11 a.m. to 6 p.rn. He has described the nature 
of work done by him. We are not concerned with the 

nature of work done by him. He was paid Rs. 1 1|2 per day. 
He worked for about 2 to 3 months with this pay. He has 
denied to have submitted in writing that he is not willing 
to work. Still however he was relieved. Till lhat date no 
document was produced by the bank and consequently no 
document was shown to him that it bears his signature. 
Thus his deposition was taken without showing the document 
to him. Hir, deposition war, recorded on 1-7-92. The depoti- 
tion of the other workman was recorded on 29-7-82. There¬ 
after on 4-12-92 that the hank had produced two documents. 
One of the documents is allegel to be the document signed 
by Ihe concerned workman. Even then the bank has not 
produced the original document. It has produced xerox 
copy of the document. As it was produced on 4-12-92 i.e. 
after the deposition of the concerned workman, consequently 
his evidence could not be recorded so far as this document 
is concerned. Shri Arunkumar R. Bhargav Px. 20 has said 
about this document. Now if we go through the written 
statement Fx. 4 it is nowhere contended that the concerned 
workman had submitted his re agnation and thus he had vol¬ 
untarily given up bis assignment as the messenger of the 
bank. It therefore appears that this defence was taken very 
lute ns the belated defence. Jt is nowhere mentioned in 
Ex. 4, It appears to have been put to the concerned workman 
on 1-7-92. He is cross-examined, However, at that time 
also the document was not with Ihe bank. It was produced 
about more than 5 months, thereafter, with the result it 
coifid not shown to the concerned workman and hence the 
concerned workman has not said lhat it bears his signature. 
Now as already said by me (he document Ex. 21 is not the 
oi'g.inal document, li is the xerox copy of the document 
and so it cannot be taken into consideration. The learned 
advocate of tile second party has drawn my attention to a 
case reported in 198-1 (I) LET at page 388, wherein it was 
held ths( tho duty of Ihe Tribunal is to confine itself to 
issues ' used by (he parties and not travel bevond such issues. 
The issues raised hv the party can be determined as a lesult 
of ci nflict of the parlies and as already said by me the 
written statement Ex. 1 doc- not conln.n the contention that 
the concerned workmen had voluntarily resigned and thereby 
abandoned the services with effect from a particular date. 

6 The conerrped workman was paid at the rate of Rs. 180 
ne” mouth. This Is what he has said in his deposition. It 
appears lhat he was a part-time employee. He had worked 
from 9-10-84 to 3-10-84. There is no question regardin'' his 
having no worker] for 240 days continuous)v in a partimlar 
calender year. Therefore, ii must he held that he had 
woilcd continuously for 2-10 days in a calendar veal". Of 
course it onpenrs that he h 'd wmked ns a part time labourer 
or a part-time employee. Even then Section 25F will have 
to be complied with before terminating his service? As tbs

THE GAZETTE OE INDIA: MARCH 27, myciIAITRA 6, 1915 [Part II— Sec. 3(H)] 


BETWEEN 


provisions contained in Section 25F are not complied with 
the termination of his services will have to be regarded as 
void ah initio. His termination was Iherelore brought about in 
breach of the mandatory requirement contained in Section 
25F of the l.D. Act, 1947. lienee such a termination can¬ 
not be recognised by law. Such termination will have to be 
regarded as void uh initio. It was submitted thaL his name 
was not registered with the employment exchange and so 
the office had not sent his name with the result that he could 
not be called for interview. We are nbi concerned with this 
reouirement. He was already in seivice. His services were 
terminated without complying with the mandatory require¬ 
ment contained in Section 25F of the l.D Ac(, 1947. There¬ 
fore his termination is void ab initio. Consequently it will 
have to be held that he continues to be in continuous service 
widi effect from the date of his termination wilh full hack 
wages of Rs, 180 per month. In the result I pass the follow¬ 
ing order. 

ORDER 

The reference is allowed and so the termination of the 
services of the concerned workman Shri Slibekhan M. Pathan 
is held to. be void ab initio. Consequently he continue'.) to 
be in continuous service with effect from the date of his 
actual termination from service at the monthly pay of 
Rs. 1R0/- per month. The first party is directed to pay 
Rs. 200 by way of cost to the second parly and bear its 
own.

AWARD 

The Government of India as per Order No. L-17012|182| 
901IR13-11, dated ad referred Inis Industrial Dispute 1 for 
adjudication to this Tribunal. 

The issue for adjudication is the following .— 

“Whether the action of the management of the Life Insu¬ 
rance Corporation of Indra, Trivandrum in removing from 
service Shri. R. Suresh, Development Officer wilh effect 
from 19-4-1989 is justified 7 

If not to what relief if, the workman entitled to ?” 

Li. The removal of Shri. Suresh was oil the basis of a 
domestic enquiry. Sri. Suresh, the workman had a case 
that there was no proper and valid domestic enquiry. There¬ 
fore tfic validity of the domestic enquriy was tried as a 
preliminary issue and by order dated 17-11-1992 this Tribu¬ 
nal found that there was a proper and valid domestic en¬ 
quiry. The necessary facta involved in this case are stated 
in that order which 1 shall extract below in full : — 

ORDER 

The removal of Sri. R. Suresh, Development Officer from 
the service of the management of Life Insurance Corpora¬ 
tion of India has led to this reference. 

2. The management, Life Insurance Corporation of India 
initiated disciplinary proceedings against the workman, Sri. 
Suresh alleging five charges as per charge sheet dated 
4-5-1987 in connection with a proposal for Rs. 5,000 on 
the life of one Sri. Sasidharan Nair, w,ho had expired about 
nine years ago on 13-9-1986. In pursuance to the charge 
memo the management ordered a domestic enquiry since 
the workman failed to respond to the charge sheet. Sri. 
M. Mukundan was the Enquiry Officer who conducted the 
domestic enquiry in which the workman participated through¬ 
out. The enquiry officer found the woikman guilty of 
charges 1 to 4, Accepting the findings of the enquiry oilicer 
the management ordered removal of the workman from 
their service. 

3. The workman in his claim statement has advanced his 
contentions which are hriefly as under : The workman 
joined the service of management in November, 1984 as a 
Probationary Development Officer. While he was working 
at Adoor he was charge sheeted alleging five charges with 
regard to a proposal for Rs. 5,000 on the life of one Sri, 
P. K. Sasidharan Nair without satisfying the identity of the 
life proposed and recommended the acceptance of the propo¬ 
sal that tho workman had issued receipt acknowledging remit¬ 
tance of Rs. 51.70 and deposited the amount at the brunch 
office of management at Punalur 'hat the workman submitted 
the S.S.L.C. Book of Sri Sasidharan Nair as age proof for the 
proposal that the workman submitted confidential report and 
issued receipt and produced age proof for existant person 
who had expired nine years ago and that the workman had 
furnished false and fabricated particulars about non existant 
person and also manipulated to forge signature on the pro¬ 
posal purpoted to be fhat of thj life proposed with 
the intenlion of deceiving the management. I he work¬ 
man was not given reasonable opportunity to defend 
his case in the enquiry and the enquiry was in a highly 
partisan manner. He was not given sufficient opportu¬ 
nity to cross examine the management witnesses and 



[will —.1 (i i ) J WTTnTT^rpm; 27, 1993/% H, 191 5 J Q31 


to give his evidence. The enquiry officer admitted docu¬ 
ments Without proving properly. Exhibit No. 15 is one 
example. Material witnesses were not examined in the 
enquiry. The enquiry oiflccr hats placed blind reliance on 
hcrexuy evidence. The enquiry was conducted violating 
principles of uuluial justice causing serious prejudice to the 
workman, The enquiry officer has not analysed the evidence 
properly before unvmg his conclusion. The findings of 
the enquiry officer was perverse, unfair and arbitrary. The 
procedure irregularities completely vitiated the enquiry. 
Charge Nos. 1 to 4 are only sub divisions of charge No, 5 
and the enquiiy officer had categorically found that charge 
No. 5 has not been proved. There is no allegation that the 
workman had obtained any monetary gain by submitting Jhe 
proposal in question and that the Corporation did not 
suiter any pecuniaty loss. According to the workman the 
punishment of removal from service is grossly dispropor¬ 
tionate and jl legal to the nature of charges and the punish¬ 
ment is unsustainable. The prayer is for reinstatement in 
servieo with backwages and all other benefits. 

4. The management in their counter has advanced their 
contentions which ate briefly as under : This Tribunal has 
no jurisdiction to entertain this dispute. The workman 
suppressed material fact's and alleged falsehood. The perfor¬ 
mance of the workman as probationary officer was not 
at all satisfactory and his probation period was extended 
for one more year. During that period lie submitted a 
piopo-al to the management for Rs. 5000 on the life of 
one Sri. P. K. Sasidharan Naif. Since the proposal was 
under non-medical scheme the workman submitted his 
confidential report about the proposer and issued temporary 
receipt acknowledging remittance of Rs. 51,70. The proposal 
form had ‘also been submitted in the handwriting of the 
workman. He also submitted S.S.1..C. Book as proof of 
age. The management relying on the confidential report 
bonafide believing that the contentions of the proposal and 
documents wore true issued a policy on the life of Sri. 
P. K. Sasidharan Nair for the sum assured of Rs. 5000. 
The policy was returned undelivered with the endorsement 
"Addresee expired—returned to the sender”. The manage¬ 
ment had received u letter from the father of the deceased 
Satiidharan Nair informing that Sri. Sasldharur Nair had 
died long back. Other enquiries of the management also 
confirmed the said fact. The workman had Infact issued a 
confidential repoit temporary receipt and ulso produced 
proof of age on a non existant person. The v'Orkman was 
therefore charge sheeted on 4-5-1984 and enquiry was 

conducted. The enquiry officer found the workman guilty 
of the 4 chargeu and the management ordered the removal 
after giving show cause notice to the workman. The en¬ 
quiry was conducted properly and strictly adhering to the 
principles of natural justice, equity and good conscience. 
The workman was granted sufficient opportunity to partici¬ 
pate in the enquiry and to cross examine the management 
wit reuses and to lead his own evidence. The enquiry officer 
fully analysed the evidence and arrived at his conclusion. 
The appeals filed by the workman were also dismissed. The 
findings of the enquiry officer was totally based on material 
evidence. There han been no procedural or substantial 
defects in holding the enquiry. The claimants deserved lo 
he dismissed from service in the light of the cases proved 
against him. Her say evidence hud not been marked the enquiry 
officer. The exhibit relied on hy the enquiry officer is admittedly 
in the handwriting of the workman. Charges numbem 
2 and 3 were admitted hv the workman. 1st. No. 15 

alone has not been relied on by the enquiry officer ns 
alleged bv the workman. Even without examining the 
Headmaster of the institution from where file S.S.L.C. Book 
of the proposed Sri Sasidharan Nair was produced and nl'n 
due to the non examination of the agent who alleg-dlv 
furnished the details of proposer Sri. Sasidharan Nair to th' 
workman the charge stood proved bevond doubt. Charges 
1 to 4 itself tantamount to mbconducla and breach of 
Pepulptions 71 74 and 30(11 of t ife Insurance Corporation 

of Tndia Staff Regulation 1060. The action of management 
terminating the service of the workman 1 is fuflv justified and 
the punishment is onlv commemorate with the misconduct 
of the workman. According lo the management the work¬ 
man is not entitled to any relief, 

5. Sirce the validity of the domestic enquiry was under 
serious attack, that point was considered on a preliminary 


basis. The enquiry file containing, show cause notice, 
proceedings of the enquiry deposition of witnesses, docu¬ 
ments and the findings of the enquiry officer have been 
marked as Ext. Ml without examining the enquiry officer 
as consented to by the learned counsel for the workman. 
The management has not examined any witnesses. The 
workman also did not adduce any evidence. 

6 . The management has raised two preliminary objections 
in the argument note filed by the management counsel. 
The first one is that the Government of India has referred 
only the removal from service of the workman and therefore 
no dispute in regard to the validity or legality or propriety 
of the domestic enquiry conducted by the management can 
now be raised. This objection according lo me is unsus¬ 
tainable as it is now settled position of law that in a case 
where the removal of a workman from the service was 
done on the basis of a domestic enquiry and the validity 
of tiie enquiry is under attack, this Tribunal has to consider 
the validity or propriety of the domestic enquiry on a 
preliminary baais and if the domestic enquiry is found to 
he invalid or vitiated the management has not the right 
to adduce fresh evidence lo prove the charge's levelled against 
the workman with the leave of the Court. 

7. The next objection is that thin Tribunal has no juris¬ 
dictions to entertain this dispute. According to learned 
counsel for the management after amendment of 1981 to 
t.hc Life Insurance C rrpoijtion Act 1956 anything contain- 
ol in the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ('he Act for short) 
or any other law or settlement or agreement arrived or olher 
instruments rnforcc cease to have any force. It is further 
submitted that after the aforementioned amendment the 
provisions of the Act has got no application in the matter 
of seivice conditions of the employees of Life Insurance Cor¬ 
poration and as r.uch this rfefercncc itself is bad in law and 
without jurisdiction. In the argument note of management re¬ 
liance was also placed on a decision of the Supreme Court re¬ 
ported in 1982 SC 1126 and also the Calcutta FEgh Court in 
Life Insurance Corporation of IV Parmal Chamda. Raha and 
others. At the outset I mnv state that the management has 
not raised any such objection in the writlen statement filed 
before this Tribunal earlier. Further the T.7.C. Act or the 
amendment mentioned above were also not made available 
to this Tribunal and established that Industrial Dispute Act 
has no application as contended. The journal in which the 
decisions mentioned above were reported, L also not stated 
in the argument note. The decisions were not made available 
to this Tribunal. Hence the decisions could not be consi¬ 
dered. The learned counsel for the workman in the argu¬ 
ment note has brought to the not.ee of this Tribunal a 
Division Bench decision of the Supreme Court in S. K. 
Varma v. Mahesh Chandra and anarhei (1983—47FLR313) 
in support of the argument that this Tribunal has jurisdic¬ 
tion to consider the issue under reference. In that decision 
the Supreme Court examined the question whether a Develop¬ 
ment Officer of Life Insurance Corporation is a workman 
within the meaning of the Act and the court held that the 
Development Officer is a workman within the meaning of 
Section) 2(s) of the Act. In the case before me also the 
question relates to a disciplinary action and removal from 
service of a Development Officer. Therefore the decision 
of the Supreme Court in S. K. Varma case settled the 
controversy here. In the light of this decision I hold that 
this Tribunal has jurisdiction to entertain .this reference) 
and this reference is maintainable. The objection raised by 
the management is unsustainable. 

8 . The first point of attack against the enquiry is that 
the enquiry was conducted in gross of violation of the 
principles of natural justice. According to the learned 
counsel for thy workman, the workman was not afforded 
ample opportunity to participate in the enquiry to cross 
examine management witnesses and to adduce evidence on 
his side. It is evident from Ext. Ml enquiry file that the 
workman was represented by a defence A.ist, in the enquiry 
and the defence Asst fully and elaborately cios3 examin¬ 
ed the witnesses examined on the side of management. The 
defence Asaistnnit submitted before the '’nquiry officer that 
the workman had no evidence to render in the enquiry. 
The workman or his Assistant never raised any objection 
regarding the person who conducted the enquiry or the 
procedure followed by the enquiiy officer. The procedure 



1032 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: MARCH 27. 1993/CHAITRA 6, 1915 [Part IT— Sec. 3(ii)l 


irregularity alleged by the workman in his claim statement 
was seen raided at any point of time during the enquiry. The 
workman and his representative has signed the day to day 
proceedings recoided by the olllccr without raising any ob¬ 
jection whatsoever. These cueumstances make it clear 
that the enquiry was conducted strictly adhering to the prin¬ 
ciples of natural justice. The contention advanced on behalf 
of the workman iru this regard is devoid of merit. 

9. Tile second point urged by the learned counsel for 
the workman ago!util enquiry is that the onquiiy officer ha. 
committed serious procedural lapse and material procedural 
irregularities in conducting the enquiry and therefore the 
enquiry is vitiated. It was pointed out by the learned 
counsel that the enquiry officer has committed a grave 
error in admitting documents without proving them properly. 
As an example the learned counsel pointed out that Ext. 
No. 15 in the enquiry which is stated to be a letter dated 
5-1-1987 received front Sri Rcghunuthun Asan, agent, who 
was working under the workman. According to the learned 
counsel this letter has been heavily relieu upon by the en¬ 
quiry officer to draw adverse interference against the work¬ 
man. 7 his letter wati not marked through Sri. Reghuuathan 
Asan but it was marked through a Senior Officer of Ihc 
management who was examined as PW2 in ihc enqtiiiy. 
Marking the documents anu relying on the contends are 
entirely diifcrent. The document in question was marked 
through a management witnesses in the mquiry and there 
was no objection, at all from the side of the workman. 
However, the enquiry officer has not placed reliance oil this 
document for arriving at his conclusion against the work¬ 
man. As a matter of fact the enquiry officer has not 
accepted thi'.* document while considering charge No. 5 and 
that charge was found in favour of Ihc wo it man. So by 
marking Ext. No, 15 in the enquiry no prejudice was 
caused to the workman and therefore it cannot be said that 
the enquiry officer has committed any procedural lapse. The 
next argument is that the enquiry officer has placed reliance 
on huresay evidence without examining two material wit¬ 
nessed though they were cited in the witness schedule. Ac¬ 
cording to the learned counsel the enquiry officer has accept¬ 
ed the statement of PW2 in the enquiry regarding the sub¬ 
mission of S.S.L.C. Book hi proof of age of Sri. Satsidharnn 
Nair as per charge No. 4 without examining the principal 
or h. D. College of Engineering, Adoor from where the 
workman allegedly procured S.S.L.C. Books ri Sri. Sa’iidharan 
Nair. Die senior branch manager as PW2 has deposed in 
the enquiry that lie was informed by the Principal that the 
workman had taken away from his institution. 3 or 4 
SSLC Books including that of Sri. Sasidharan Nair. The 
submission of SSLC Book by the workman before the 
management is not in dispute. There was also no sugges¬ 
tion lo PW2 in the enquiry that he had given false evi¬ 
dence to trap the workman due to enemby or any other 
reason. Further, on going through the deposition of PW2 
there are no reasons to disbelieve the statement of a senior 
branch manager of the management without any reason what¬ 
soever. Therefore the non examination oi the principal of 
the college is not material. The enquiry officer has not 
committed any procedural lapse in relying on the evidence 
of PW2 and there is no procedure irregularity. 

10. The third point urged by the learned counesl for the 
workman is that charge No. 5 is ihc sum and substance of 
all the charges and the enquiry officer has found charge 
No. 5 not proved against the workman. Therefore accord¬ 
ing to the learned counsel the findings of the enquiry officer 
that the workman is cuiltv of charges l to 4 ore not at all 
sustainable. Tt is true that all charges are ba*>cd on one 
incident. But it cannot he said that the charges arc one 
and the same. On going through the clutges it is clear 
that the charges are distinct and separate. So the conten¬ 
tion that since charge No. 5 was not proved against the 
workman, the workman cannot he stated 'o be guilty of 
charges 1 to 4 is without force. 

11. Charge No. 1 relates to submission of confidential 
report in form No. 3251 on the proposal for Rs 5,000 on 
the life of Sri. P. K. Sasidharan Nair without satisfying about 
the identity of the life proposed. The workman has admitt¬ 
ed the handwriting in form No. 3251 submitted by h T m. He 
has certified the identity of the person propo-^ 1 for insurance. 
He has answered the question in the form slaimg that he 


had satisfied about the identity of the life proposed. At 
that time life proposed Sri. Sasidharan Nair was already 
dead. That itself shows that the workman had g'ven the 
answer's without any enquiry or investigation. It is not 
disputed that as Development Officer he is duty bound to 
send this vital report in form 3251 only after conducting 
proper and discreat enquiiy and satisfying h.ir-rli about the 
identity of the proponent. The learned counsel for the work¬

lo the workman. According to the learned counsel the

iden ity of the person and other details. Il may he remem¬

is very much clear from these aspects. So the present con¬ 
tention can be considered only as rn af cr thought. The en¬ 
quiry officer has analysed the evidence propci lv and came 
to his conclusion finding the workman guilty of charge No. 1, 

12. Charge Nos. 2 and 3 were admitted by the workman 
and on the basis of the admission the enquiry officer entered

Charge No, 4 was also proved agaimt the workman, ["he

guilty of charge No. 4. 

13. The workman has a contention that he was a stranger

ground of the persons and about the area of hits jurisdiction.

the workman had committed grave dereliction of duly that

Insurance Corporation. There is yet another contention

in enquiry information pertaining to special reports for all

him he is not guilty of dereliction of duty. Hut he has no

Development Officer has to conduct enquiry of a particular

of work he could have taken more time and discharg¬

tention is also without force. 

14. The workman as per his letter dated KS-2-1987. to ihe 
management admitted that he has answered the identity 
of th*- life proposed in the affirmative that he bad recommend¬

along with the evidence adduced in the cnqu'iv and the

findings of the enquiry officer fully establish that the work¬

13. fit the result, I hold that the enquiry was conducted

III. The only question now remains for consideration it. 
regarding the pioprietory of punishment imposed by the 
management. The learned counsel lor the management would

mbconduiict proved against him the management happened

workman was cnrelcw; in securing ’ho pioposnl which result¬ 
ed in the issurancc of the policy in the name of a dead

plaint against him during that period except the present 
charge. But the misconduct happened due to his Careless¬ 
ness only as admitted by the senior branch manager. On 
an anxious consideration of all these aspects I am of the 
view that the punishment of dem. il F to 0 harsh to be 
sustained. The management failed io consider ihcsc aspects 
and failed to award a lesser puiMshrncut However, the 
workman cannot be let off without any punishment for the

reimtated in service, 

V. In the result, an award is passed dimcvug the manage¬ 
ment to reinstate the workman, Sri. R. Suresh in the service 

of the management without backwages and any other

This referece is referred by Government of TnJia, Ministry 
of Labour vide letter No. D12012/270 / 89-D.II(A) dated 
20-11-1989 for adjudication of the dispute between the Man¬ 
agement of Syndicate Bank and their workman with the 
following Anncxure : 

“Whether the action of the management of Syndicate 
Bank in dismissing from service Shri A. Venkatar.ro 
is justified ? If not. to what relief is the workman 
entitled 7" 

2 The above reference is registered as T.D. No. 86 of 1989 
in ibis Tribunal and noticee were sent lo both sides. The

workman filed claim statement, and the brief contents of the 
same arc as follows ; 

It is stated by the workman that he is Questioning the 
illegal dismissal of the workman, who is a member of tht 
Union and worker of the respondent industry, the petitioner — 
Union taking up the cause raised the above l.D. Sri A. 
Venkalarao was appointed as attender in Syndicate Hank in 
its Yenkatagiri branch, Ncllore District. The workman was 
discharging his duties to the entire satisfaction of the res¬ 
pondent without any complaints of whatsoever. While so, 
Sri A. Vcukatarao was issued a charge memo dated 10-12-82 
making allegation that he misappropriated a sum of Rs. 45.Ho 
which was received by him for the purpose of payment of 
Telegram bill and the workman fraudonrly withdrew a sum 
of Rs. 45 from the S.B. Account on 13-11-1981 and the 
3rd charge is Sri Venkatarao picked up 20 rupee note on 
12-4-82 from the cash cabin. The said allegations arc not 
correct, Sri A. Venkatarao submitted his explanation with 
regard to the first, charge. lie stated that he foigot to 
send tlie Telegram since her had gone to his native place on

after himself returned to duty. With regard to second charge 
that he submitted and admitted about the incident with 
regard to drawal of Rs. 45 from the S.B. Account book for 
his benefit. With regard to 3rd charge the workman had 
emphatically denied and while he attempted to hand over 
Rs. 20 note to the Branch Manager he was forcibly taken 
by the Assistant Branch Manager and such allegation was 
levelled against him. The workman was never had any in¬ 
tention to misappropriate the funds and the workman being 
a sub-staff and uneducated such mistake were committed. It 
i s further stated that his explanation to the management is 
not properly considered and mechanical regular manner while

Office* had not conducted enquiry fairly. The enquiry is 
illegal and violation of principles of natural justice. The 
Enquiry Officer conducted the enquiry only against 3rd 
charge i.e. with regard to the allged theft of 20 rupee note 
for which he denied the charge. Enquiry Officer should 
have conducted the enquiry against all the three charges but 
he conducted against only one charge leaving the first and 
second charges against him. The Enquiry Officer did not 
furnish the list of witnesses who are going to be examined 
iu the enquiry. The workman may not engage any counsel 
to leport his case. With regard to the second charge although 
he admitted but the amount was recredited to the account 
of the persOn who is holding the Pass Book. There is no 
evidence with regard to the misappropriation of funds. The 
second charge which shows that he committed forgery is 
not made out. There is no complaint, given by the account 
holder Smt. J. VisulaWinnua in this regard. The Manager 
was also not collected the sample thumb impression of the 
S.B. Account holder. These facts have not been produced 
before the Enquiry Officer. 

It is further slated that Smt. Bhoolaxmi, Cashier had given 
him a chit bearing 20 rupee note and the same was obtained 
from the workman. There is no documentary evidence to 
consider that the currency note was seized. There is no 
Panchanama to that effect. The chit allegedly given by the 
Cashier to the Manager was also nor produced before the 
Enquiry Officer. The Cashier statea thai she had not noticed 
the workman taking the currency note from the d.awer of 
the cash cabin. The evidences placed before the Enquiry 
Officer and their charges are not properly appreciated by 
the Enquiry Officer. The enquiry is illegal and sufficient 
opportunity was not given to tlw workman during the course 
of the enquiry. Dismissal order is illegal void and he also 
staled that the punishment of removal from service is dis¬ 
proportionate to the proved charges. 

3. On the other hand the management filed the Counter 
wheiein it contends that the material averments of the claim's 
statement are not correct. Tt cannot laid that ihc cnqir'rv 
was no conducted in fair manner. The workman was involv¬ 
ed in fraudulent withdrawal of money from the custnnvTs 
account. Therefore he is involved in dishonesty and moral 
turpitude which tarnished the image of the respondent hank 
if the petitioner is allowed to continue in the cervices of 
the bunk. It is not correct to say that thpunishment of 
dismissal is disproncrHonrire to the proved charges. Wi*h re¬ 
gard to the non-Payment of Rs. 43 towards tclenrum the 
contention of (be workman is ibat he failed to issue Telegram 
by oversight as lie had been to lus native place on some 


urgent domestic work. But only he was remainded on 20-1-81 
he did not remit the amount. With regard to the second charge 
jc is az.io the proved fact and the workman himself admitted 
Unit he withdrew the amount. The workman clearly admitted 
that charges No. 1 and 2 are true. He admitted the guilt and 
he also gave reasoning for committing such guilt as on account 
of his family problems. 1 he workman voluntarily admitted 
guilt, it is not correct to state that the enquiry officer was 
only relied upon the admissions made by the petitioner, but 
also conducted the enquiry by following the basic principles 
of natural justice. Sufficient opportunity was given to the 
workman to cross-examine the witnesses and all the copies 
of the documents have been furnished to the workman. Sim¬ 
ply because the workman did not reimburse Rs. 45.80 ps. 
which false under first charge the reason stated by him that 
he lorgotlcu due to domestic affairs is not acceptable. Only 
after Manager reminds he did not remit bank Telegram 
chaiges. He is silent in the matter as pointed against him 
and il he really innocent he should have brought to the 
notice of the Manager about that amount. 

W th regard to the second charge of 45 rupees withdrawal 
fiom the is.ii. Account of one Smt. J. VisalaLshmmu the 
same chargo was admitted by the workman himself and 
because that he recredrted Rs. 45 to the account number of 
Smt. J. Visalakshamma. The guilt of the v otkman cannot 
be disproved, t he workman knows the consequences of the 
m v.onduct. With regard to Charge No. 3 Sri S. S. Bhatt 
gave in his evidence who is examined as M.W. 1 that 
Venkatruo in spite o* repealed lequests denied that he had 
taken the note and finully showed him the slip containing 
the uole number then he surrendered the note and he verified 
the cash and found 20 rupee note was missing and in that 
bundle 9 pieces were there instead ot 100 pieces. When once 
the workman was possessing 20 rupee note and not revealing 
the fact when it was enquired by the Assistant Manager it 
is nothing but dishonest on the part of the workman. Unless 
the branch manager forcibly taken 20 rupee rote he ought 
not have surrendered the note. The Murtger staled that no 
proof in the contention of ihe workman that enquiry was 
not conducted in accordance with the Principles of natural 
justice. It is not correct to contend that the Management 
mechanically accepted the finding of the enquiry report. The 
management examined all tho material facts involved in the 
enquiry report and having considered the gravity of the nature 
of the misconduct committed by the workman the Manage¬ 
ment imposed capital punishment of dismissing workman 
from service. The ref oi'b ;h« v/orktnnn was dismissed trom 
the service. The management further contended fliat the 
contention of tho workman that the punishment of dismissal 
from service is disproportionate lo the proved charges is 
untenable, 'therefore the workman is not entitled re get 
any lelief and award may be passed accordingly. 

4. No oral evidence was addued by bolh sides. Exhibits 
Ml to M23 are marked by consent. This Tribunal gave 
find nig on the assessment of as to whether the enquiry held 
valid or not that it was held valid. The aiguments of the 
both sides are beard. The learned advocate for (he workman 
argued that only ono point before this Tribunal i.e past 
conduct of the workman is without any punishments. It is 
tunher argued that the workman was involved in 3 charges. 
First charge was with regard to non-payment of Telegram 
charges for Rs. 45.80 Ps. That Dmount was repaid by the 
workman subsequently. The second charge is with regald 
to the drawal of Rs. 45/- from the S. B. Account of one acco¬ 
unt holder by name Smt. J. Visalakashamma and the same was 
holder by name Smt. T. Visalakshamma and the same was 
also admitted by the workman end recreditecl the amount of 
Rs. 45 to the account of Smt .1. Visaiakshanmia. The Third 
charge is with regard to attempt to commit fraud of 20 rupee 
note from the cash cabin that was recovered from him. Eet 
us assume that workman committed all the 3 offences. But 
the amount involved in all the three offences put together 
is only aboul Rs, 100. Whether it Rs. 100 or Rs. 1.000 
offence is a offence and fraud is a fraud. But il is the duty 
of the Management to go through the past record of the 
iinkman in question, Tf the past record is very bad and if 
there are any incidents of this nature in the past seivicc 
of the workman certainly it is a point against the workman. 
In such cases the workman deserves no sympalhv. But in 
this case previous record of the workman is clean, offences 
proved involving only about Rs. 100 and it is evident that 
the dismissal from service for the said proved charges wherein 
involvement of about Rs. 100 is disptoportionate. Therefore 
the dismissal order may be set aside. 



[«TT<T II — m 3 (il)] T'l 

On the other hand the learned advocate for the respondent 
argued that absolutely there is no point in favour of the 
workman on the following grounds. The workman is in¬ 
volved in proved misconduct of misappropriation of public 
funds cheating and also forgery. Simply because the past 
record of the workman is without any punishment the voik- 
man is not entitled to receive mercy in the hands of the 
banking authorities. The barking authorities taken into 
account of the gravity of die provtd chatges of 
cheating, forgery and misappropriation of public funds. 
Simply because the 20 rupee note was recovered 
Rs. 45.80, Rs. 45 were credited to the funds of the respec¬ 
tive accounts the workman cannot claim that he is not in¬ 
volved in such misconduct or forgery or cheating. He further 
argued that if the workman of thi’s nature ate allowed to 
continuo in the Bank the public will loose confidence and 
it affects the turn out of bank business Cheating, forgery 
and dishonestly misappropriation of public funds are not 
small charges and their gravity in very much high and as 
such the respondent bank imposed capital punishment of 
dismissal from service. Therefore the workman is not en¬ 
titled to get an?.' relief and the award mav be passed 
accordingly, 

5. The point for consideration is whether there are any 
valid grounds to confirm the dismissal order passed against 
A, Venkatarao by the management is justified or not? 

6. At the very out set I would like to mention that it 
is the admitted fact by both sides that the workman was 
involved in misconduct of misappropriation of public funds 
forgery anti also cheating. Rs. 45.80 Ps. which was entrust¬ 
ed t 0 him by the Manager for issuing the Telegram is mis¬ 
appropriated by the workman. But when it was enquired 
by the management he credited the same to the funds of 
the management and gave some explanation, The second 
charge is cheating the person who is having account in their 
Bank by withdrawing Rs. 45. It is an admitted fact by the 
workman himself, and there is no cxcusion for the same 
as rightly argued by learned advocate for the respondent. 
With regard to the 3rd charge it is only attempt to fraud 
and fraud in particular and the 20 rupee note in question 
was recovered from the possession of the workman. With 
regard to the first charge the workman gave reasoning that 
he left for his home village on account of urgent message 
received by him and failed to isMie Telegram, With regard 
to the second choree he stated that on account of his 
domestic problems be was forced to do so and the amount 
in question was credited to the funds of the S.B. Account 
of the person who is holding the account. 

7. I would like to further mention that the quantum of 
money Involved either in the cheating or misappropriation 
of public funds is only about Rs. 85.80 Fs. and it is not 
in thousands or lakhs. Here I would like to mention that 
whether the amount involved in question is Rn. 10. Rs, 100, 

Rs. 1,000 or Rs. 1 lakh is not the point in issue hut Ihe 
nature of the offence is the point in issue. Although the 
workman involved in minor amount of misappropriation of 
public funds cheating a person does not mean that the 
gravity of the proved facts of the workman is reduced to a 
greater extent. 

8. As per the service conditions of the Syndicate Bank 
under Section 12 sub-section (C) “In awarding punishment 
by way of disciplinary action the aulhornv conccred shall 
take into account the gravity of the misconduct the previous 
record, if any. of the employee, and any olher aggravating 
or extenuating the circumutarccs. that may exi t. Where suffi¬ 
cient extenuating circumstances exist, the misconduct mav 
be condoned and in case such misconduct is of the “gross" 
type, be may be merely discharged, with or without^ notice 
or on payment of a month’s pay and allowance in lieu of 
notice. 

9. In this case the Standing Orders of the service condi¬ 
tions of the R-cspofideni Bank clearly shows the previous 
service record of the workman should nbo be taken inlo 
account wh ; le imposing punishment Tile Management con¬ 
tention in that Ihev have also taken the above issue into the 
cmideration but they only gave proper pumshtront in the 
said circumstances is nothing but dismissal irom service. 


TT r R : mnj 27, lhyj fa 6, 1 a 1 S l()3i 

In this connection I would like to mention that it is an 
admitted fact that the previous entire service of the work¬ 
man is without any punishment and he did jot involve m 
any offence of this nature previously. Jf the workman is in¬ 
volved once or twice in the offence of this nature previously 
during his tenure of service certainly the workman did not 
deserve any (sympathy or mercy of the management. But 
here in this case il is clear that the previous service of 
the workman is clear and without blame. He also gave 
reasoning that his hard domestic circumstances forced to 
misappropriate and cheat Rs. 45 and also Rs. 45.80 Ps. under 
the charges one and two respectively. In view of ihe above 
facts and circumstances I am of the clear opinion that this 
Tribunal with quantum of punishment interfere under Sec¬ 
tion 11A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and I consider 
that the capital punishment for dismissal from service in 
disproportion to the proved charges against the workman 
and l am of the clear opinion that the ends of the justice 
will be met if the workman will he appointed ia the bank 
as Attender without any back wage 1 .;. He is entitled for atten¬ 
dant benefits. 

10. In the result I find that the action of the management 
of Syndicate Bank in dismissing from serv"’e Shri A. Ven- 
katarno is not justified. The respondent is directed to rein¬ 
state the workman, The workman is not entitled to get 
any back wages but he is entitled for attendant benefits. 

11. Award passed accordingly. 

Y. VENKATACTfALAM, Chairman

fied in termination the services of Sri Deep Kumar 
Bhuriya w.e.f. 26-6-85 'n violation of section 25G

workman is entitled ? 

In this case vide order dt. 5-6-9 i the appearance of Sri 
M. Shakeel as authorised repnwwitn.tive for the workman was 
disallowed, and the case was ordered to come up for the 
ctoss examination of the workman on 5-8-91. Since 5-8-91 
till 26-5-92 the workman did not appeared in the case. On

ed on account of paucity of time, to 13-10-92. On 13-10-92

has not filed the annexurcs referred to In his affidavit and

needful by 7-12-92 and the case was adjourned to 9-12-92.

Shakeel had already been debarred from appearing from the 
side of the workman vide order dt 5-6-9j, therefore h’S 
presence on 3-2-93 has no meaning. 

3. Tn view of the facts and circumstances of the case it 
appears that the workman a.! not Interested in prosecuting his 
cir-e. Therefore a no claim award is given in the case against 
the workman,

AWARD 

In this reference made by the Hon’ble Central Government 
by its order No. T.-]2012/93/92-IR(B,n) dt. 20-J-92 under 
Sec. 10(2A)(l)(d) of ID. Act the point for adjudication its 
per schedule to reference is : 


2. Id this esae the claimant is Smt. Bhagya Laxmi, wife 
of late M. Shivaramegouda (wrongly slated as M. Shivaram 
in the reference) and Shivarnmegouda was clerk in the 11 
patty bank. In respect of the charge sheet dt. 12-12-87 against 
Shivaramegouda, there was a D.E, against him. The findings 
of the enquiry officer that the charges against Shivaramegouda 
were proved were accepted by the disciplinary authority and 
ultimately he was dismissed on 30-11-88. 

3. The charges against said Shivaramegouda were :— 

(i) Tlmt Shivaramegouda floated a firm under the name 

and style M/s. Manjunatha Enterprises at Mysoio 
with his wife Bhagya Laxmi (claimant) and his other 
close relatives as partners. The said firm was carry¬ 
ing on business ol distributing prizes such ns T.V. 
sets, Luna etc., by lucky dip. 

(ii) That said Shivaramegouda. by misusing his position 
ns a clerk in the IT party bank induced several cons¬ 
tituents of the bank to subscribe to the hu'iine’is 
floated by him and his relatives and collected huge 
amounts from them. Further, he cheated several 
constituents of the bank either by not returning 
the amount's collected from them or by not deliver¬ 
ing any articles after collecting money from (him. 

4. As I have already stated there was a D.E. against Shiva¬ 
ramegouda and he was dismissed- 

5. The dispute han been raised by the claimant Smt. 
Bhagya I nxmi wife of Shivaramegouda. In the claim petition 
the claimant has contended that the D.E. held against her 
husband is invalid. The order dismissing him from service 
is illegal. The order of dismksal is disproportionate to the 
allege,) mis conduct against late Shivaramegouda. It is prayed 
that the T party (wife 1 ) should be appointed on compassionate 
grounds as a clerk. 

6. In the counter statement it iu contended that the D.E. 
held against Shi vara megoudaT*. valid and proper. The punish¬ 
ment of dismissal is proportionate to the gravity of the 
mis-conduct. Shivaramegouda was disnuVcd w.c.f. 30-11-88. 
Appointment on compassionate grounds are made only when 
the employee dies While In service of the bank. In the Ins¬ 
tant case Shivaramegouda died ill the accident on 12-1-92. 

7. Evidence has not been recorded in caue. The Learned 
counsel fo r the T party has filed the memo conceeding that 
the D F. was fair and proper. He has filed another memo dt. 
17-2-93 that the I party does not challenge ihe perversity 
or otherwise of the findings of the report. 

8. The Learned Advocate for the T party argued that the 
punishmem AjSgjid on Shivaramegouda wau dhpropor- 
tfonate toTMSilBigifthde of the misconduct. The order of 
dismivsalNHfGKe misconduct. The order of dismisal should 
be set atSMe. ' • . 

> ,.■# -s- . ' ' 

9. For the limited purpose of seeing whether the punish¬ 
ment on ShivaramegOudn was adequate or not I will look 
into the charge sheet. So fas as charge 1 is concerned it 
does not Kay who were the other close relative; of Shiva¬ 
ramegouda wh6 were partners of M|s. Manjuuathi Enter¬ 
prises. So far as the IT charge Is concerned though it stated 
huge amounts were collected by Shivaramegouda it docn not 
mention even approximately the amount collected by Shiva- 
ntmegouda. It doe= nol mentibn the names of (he customers, 
except one Agarwnl, from whom ho collected amounts and 
cheated. 

10. Even with regard to the amount collected by Shiva¬ 

ramegouda from Agarwfil it was a meagre sum of Rs. 1800|-, 
even if w beh'ev ftuth e averment made in the counter state¬ 
ment. Jt. has beeA*B& down bv the Supreme Court in AIR 
1989 S.C. page outer Indin Ltd, Lucknow VK Labour 

Court. Lucknow) that "though disciplinary enquiry is found 



1045 


[STPT II— WT 3 (ii)] 



to be fait ana lawful and Its fudlttgs tyetef not vitiated ft any 
Wanner, tH£( By itself Vydffid rtOt W I gfbuifff fdt n£iif-ifrter- 
ference Of the ordtft of ffefiniifetlOfi of service”. Jbi SdfJfeihe 
Court has beeh flealed to ltiy aototf in (Bis ijutfitffity (hit 
the erring wOr^fflan should be givetl an opportunity to reform 
himself ahd prove to he loyal ahd disciplined employee. 

ff. it it abundantly Clear frOfn the niateriai on record 
that the Bank has not Suffered in any way from the alleg¬ 
ed misconduct Of Shivaramegouda. 

12. For the aforesaid reflsOnS I am of opinion that the 
Order of dismissal passed by the II party, dismissing Shivara- 
megouda is disproportionate to the nature or magnitude of 
the misconduct committee by him. If back wages are denied 
that itself will be a punishment. 

13. For the aforesaid reasons I set aside the order of dis¬ 
missal of Shivaramegouda. If he were Alive, he was entitled 
to be reinstated, 

14. The claimant is Smt. Bhagya Laxmi, wife of late 
Shivaramegouda. She has prayed that she should be append¬ 
ed In the II party Bank on compassionate grounds. The 
Learned counsel for the I party has produced zerox copy of 
the circular of II party as per thei provisions of which ap¬ 
pointments on compassionate grounds are made. The learned 
counsel for the II party did not dispute that II party has 
got power as per circular to make appointment on compas¬ 
sionate grounds. It is argued by the learned counsel for the 
II party that this Tribunal cannot give a finding that the I 
party is entitled for appointment on compassionate grounds 
and at best this Tribunal can only direct the II party ten 
consider the case of I party for appointment on compas¬ 
sionate grounds- This ’argument cannot be accepted (since the 
reference clearly ditects this Tribunal to give a finding whe¬ 
ther or not the T party wife of late Shivaramegouda, is en¬ 
titled for appointment on compassionate grounds in the 
Bank. 

15. It has been laid down by the Gujarath High Court in 
1990 (1) L.L.L 132 tDivya Ben D’Oza v/s. Divisional Cont¬ 
roller, State Road Transport Corporation, Ahmedabad) that 
noliev to give appointment on compassionate grounds should 
be liberally construed. In the authority Of the Supreme Court 
(Scooter Tndia Ltd., v|s, Labour Court, Lucknow) referred 
to above, it has been laid down by the Supreme Court that 
Labour Court can temper justice with mercy. 

4 

16. It is averred in the counter statement that Shivarame- 
gouda died ns a result of the accident, subsequent to dis¬ 
missal and so I party is not entitled to appointment. Thra 
argument does not hold water. Once the order of dismissal 
is set aside the reinstatement ordered with continuity of ser¬ 
vice, the employee must be deemed to be in service on the 
date of dismissal. 

17. Tt is not disputed that the I party Smt. Rhagva Laxmi, 
wife of the deceased workman, has completed B.A., degree 
course. It is stated in the claim petition that she has no 
means of maintenance and she has to givp education to her 
two children and She is in extreme difficulties. Bearing this 
aspect in mind, I pass the following order : 

ORDER 

It is declared that the action of the management of II party 
in dismissing M. Shivaramegouda from service was not jus¬ 
tified. The I party ‘Bhagya Laxmi is entitled for appointment 
on compassionate grounds in the bank. No backawges. Award 
passed accepting the reference as stated herein. Submit to 
Government,

S.O. 667.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act. 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the Award of the labour Court, .Kozhikode 
as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between 
the employers in relation to the management of South Mala¬ 
bar Gramin Bank and their workmen, which was received by 
the Central Government on the 3rd March, 1993, 

[L-12012/159/91-IR (B.II1)]| 
K. V. B. UfrNY, Desk Officer 

ANNEXURi 

IN THE LABOUR COURT, KOZHIKODE, KERALA 
STATE 

Dated this the 17th day of February, 1993 
PRESBNf : 

Shri K. G. Gopalakrishnan, B.A..B.L., Presiding Officer. 
f.D. (C) fro. 3/91 
BETWEEN 

Tho Chairman, 

M/s. South Malabar Gramin Bank, 

H.O. Malappuram, 

Kerala State-676505. ...Management. 

AND 

The General Secretary, 

South Malabar Gfamin Bank 

Employees Union, 

Post Box No. 16, 

Up Hill, Malappuram-676505. .. .Union, 

REPRESENTATIONS : 

Sri K. V. Sachidabandan, Adcoate, Calicut—For Manage¬ 
ment. 

Sri M. Asokan, Advocate, Calicut—For Union. 

AWARD 

By the Order No. L-12012/139/91-IR. B.I1I tho dispute 
between the aforesaid parties in connection with the justi¬ 
fiability of the imposition of a punishment of recovery of 
50 per cent of the loss of gold ornaments from the salary 
of a junior clerk by name Sri P. Krishnan was referred to 
this court for adjudication by the Central Government under 
Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act. 

2. After the receipt of the reference order in this court, 
pursuant to the notice issued from this court both the manage¬ 
ment and the union entered appearance and filed claim state¬ 
ments setting forth their stand in the dispute. The brief 
avernments in the statement filed by the union are as 
follows :—The worker P, Krishnan joined the service of (he 
management bank as a lu’nior Clerk and he was working as 
such in its Chakkittupara Branch. While so in August 1987 ho 
was put in charge of the Security locker in the bank along with 
the Manager and ho was entrusted with the second key of the 
locker. While so on 31st August, 1987, 41.800 grams of gold 
pledged by one K. Ragbavan, on 21st Januaty, 1987 was 
found missing from the locker. Though a police complaint 
was preferred bv the management the missing articles cannot 
be traced out, But however just to fix the liability on some¬ 
body the worker was proceeded against by the management 
by issuing him a memo of charges slating that as he was in 
charge of the second key of the locker at the time of the 
detection of the theft he is accountable for the loss, Though 
the worker has given a prompt reply pleading innocence and 
that he cannot be held liable, the management was not satis- 





1046 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: MARCH 27, 1993/CHAITRA 6, 1915 [Part II—Sec. 3 


find with that explanation and accordingly a domestic enquiry 
was conducted by the management in this matter, This en¬ 
quiry was in fact conducted violating all norms of natural 
justice as the worker was not given proper opportunity to de¬ 
fend himself in that enquiry. Later the Enquiry Officer gave a 
t.-orl to the management holding the worker accountable for 
I he loss and basing on that finding 50 per cent of the value 
of the said missing article was ordered to be recovered from 
the salary of the worker as punishment This action of the 
management is illegal and unjust as there is absolutely no 
evidence to say that the gold was actually lost during the 
period when the worker was in charge of the second key. 
Moreover the finding of the Enquiry Officer is absolutely per¬ 
verse as it is not at all based on any evidence. Hence the 
action of the management directing the worker to pay 50 
per cent of the value of the gold is unsustainable. An award 
may therefore be passed setting aside this action of the man¬ 
agement. 

3. The brief averments in the statement filed by the man¬ 
agement are as follows :—It is true that the worker 
P. Krishnan who is employed as a worker in the manage¬ 
ment bank was directed to pay 50 per cent of the value of 
gold which was found missing from the locker on 31st 
August, 1987. This order is passed as at the time of the 
detection of the theft he was in charge of the second key 
of the locker, the Manager being in charge of the first key. 
Thu s as the serond key holder it is the responsibility of the 
worker to keep all the securities in the security locker in 
safe custody, fn this connection it is pertinent to mention 
that for this duty he is paid extr a allowance. Thus as the 
worker was in charge of the second key of the locker at the 
time of the detection of the theft he is accountable to the 
bank for the loss sustained to the bank. But however only 50 
per cent of its valhe was ordered to be recovered from him 
and the balance 50 per ceint was recovered from the Manager 
who is in charge of the first key. That apart the Manager 
was also given another punishment of cutting of increment. 
Although tlie fact that the worker was in charge of the second 
key at the time of the detection of the theft is admitted by 
the woikcr still the management did not proceed against him 
straight away and he was asked to pay 50 per cent of its 
value only after conducting a domestic enquiry in which he . 
was found guilty. The allegation that this enquiry was con¬ 
ducted violating all norms of natural justice is absolutely 
false. Tn fact the worker was given all opportunity to state 
his case and defend himself in that enquiry. Hence an award 
may be passed ratifying this action of the management. 

4. On these pleadings the following points are formulated 
for decision :— 

(1) Whether the domestic enquiry conducted by the 
management is valid and legal ? 

(2) Whethcd tile: finding of the Enquiry Officer holding 
the worker accountable for the loss along with the 
Manager is sustainable ? 

(3) What is the proper punishment if any to be awarded 
to the worker 

(4) Result 7 

5. The evidence consists of the oral testimony of MWl 
and the documents marked as Exts. Ml and M2. 

6 . Point No. 1.—This point with regard to the validity of 
domestic enquiry was decided by me as a preliminary point 
by order dated 8th July, 1992 holding that the domestic 
enquiry is valid and legal. 

7. Point No. 2,—As stated earlier the' charge against the 
worker is that when the worker was in charge of the second 
key of the security locker, 41.800 grams of gold pledged by 
one K. Raghavan and which was kept in this locker was found 
missing on list August, 1987. According to the management 
there are 2 keys for thi s locker one with the Manager and 
another with the worker and as it can be opened only with 
these 2 keys, both the Manager and the worker are account¬ 
able for the Mss sustained to the bank on this count and ac¬ 
cordingly 50 per cent of the value of the lost gold was order¬ 
ed to be paid by the worker and the other 50 per cent was 
ordered to be paid by the Manager. Now it Is this action of 
the management asking the worker to pay 50 per cent of its 
vjlue that is called in question in this reference by the union. 


8 . Any way on fact there is absolutely no dispute be 
the parties. It is the admitted fact that 41.800 grams o! 
pledged by one K. Raghavan on 21st January, 1987 
whom was kept in the security- locker was found missing 
31st August, 1987 when a physical verification of securi 
was done on 31st August, 1987. It is also the udmitted c a. 
that from 17th August, 1987 to 7th September, 1987 the 
worker was the custodian of the second key of the locker 
and the first key was with the Manager. It is also the ad¬ 
mitted case that this locker can be opened by putting both the 
keys simultaneously and hence if either of the key is missing 
it cannot be opened. Now the stand of the management is 
that as the worker is admittedly the custodian of the second 
key at the time of the detection of the theft he is account¬ 
able for the loss. But the grievance of the worker is that 
he cannot be held liable since it is not the date of detection 
of the theft that is material but the date of actual theft- 
According to union as it was pledged on 21st January, 1987 
and in as much as it was found missing only on 31st August, 
1987 the theft can be any day in between 21st January, 1987 
and 31st August, 1987 and hence the worker who was put 
in charge of the second key only on 17th August, 1987 can¬ 
not be held liable on the grouty that at the time of the 
detection of the theft he was in charge of the second kev 
since theft can be prior to 17th August, 1987 as well. This 
is exactly his stand before the Enquiry Officer also but the 
Enquiry Officer after evaluating the evidence produced before 
him by the management over ruled this objection and found 
him accountable for the loss. Now let me sec how far this 
finding is correct. 

9. Any way I do not have to evaluate the evidence in 
detail, since as stated earlier on facts there is absolutely no 
dispute between the parties. Or in other words as observed 
earlier the controversy is only when the date of actual theft 
is not known whether the person who was in charge at the 
lime of the detection of the theft can be proceeded against. 
Though the stand of the union that the worker cannot be 
held accountable unless and unlil it is proved that the theft 
actually occurred when he was in charge of the second key 
carries little bit of weight, it cannot be accepted since ad¬ 
mittedly me worker was in charge of the second key from 
17th August, 1987 to 7th Septemhed, 1987. This is furher cleat 
from Ext. M2 Key Register. Concededly this theft was de¬ 
tected on 31st August, 1987 i.W. at a time when the worker 
was in charge of the second key. If really the theft is piior 
to 17th August. 1987 though detected only on 31st August, 
1987 as now allegedly by the union, the worker would have 
detected this on 17th August, 1987 itself when he was put 
in charge of the second key since as per rules he has to physi¬ 
cally verify each and every articles kept in the security locker 
before taking charge of the second key. Whether he has 
actually verified before taking charge of the second key is a 
different matter. If he has taken charge of the second key 
without actually verifying the locker hw has done it at his 
risk. Any way from this, one thing is obvious that the theft 
wa 9 detected at a time when the worker was in charge of 
the second key namely after 17th August, 1987, If really it 
was prior to 17th August, 1987 the worker would have detect¬ 
ed it when he was put in charge of the second key on 17th 
August, 1987. Thus I find clinching evidence to conclude 
that 41.800 grams of gold was stolen from tlie locker at a 
time when the worker was in charge of the second key. Ac¬ 
cordingly T confirm the finding of the jEhquiry Officer hold¬ 
ing him accountable for the loss. 

10. Point No. 3.—Then comes the question of punishment. 
Strictly speaking it is not a punishment because the man¬ 
agement has not taken any punitive action against the worker 
and all (bat the management has done is that the worker was 
asked to pay 50 per cent of the gold thus lost in monthly 
instalments from his salary. Thus the management was very 
lenient to him although as the person in charge of the 
second kev of the locker he is personally accountable for the 
los-, along with the Manager, So much so this action of the 
management does not call for any interfedence. 

11. In the result an award is passed fully ratifying the 
action of the management. 

12. This award will come into force 30 days after its publi¬ 
cation in (he official gazette, 

nictated to the Confidential Assistant, transcribed by him, 
revised, corrected and passed by me on the 17th day of 
February, 1993. 

K, G. GOPALAKRISHNAN, Presiding Officer

2. Shorn of all details, the case of the workman Is that 
duriDg course of employment under the first party manage¬ 
ment he met with an accident as a result he became par¬ 
tially disabled. Ke approached the Commissioner under the 
Workmen's Compensation Act and the Hon’ble High Court 
of Orissa at different times and certain orders were passed, 
in his favour by the Commissioner as well as the Hon’blil 
Court. He was ordered by the Commissioner to be taken 
back in service but it was not carried out. Again he filed 
a complaint in the Hon'blc High Court which was register¬ 
ed as Mric. Case No. 2 of 1984 where a direction was given 
that he hhould approach the employer wilhin one week, 
from the date of judgement for reinstatement and if he was 
not reinstated he might seek relief according to law. There¬ 
after he knocked at the door of the Commissioner under 
the Workmen's Compensation Act in 1985 alleging that 
despite of his repeated approach he hud been denied of emp¬ 
loyment. Evidence both oral and documentary were led 
in the proceeding and the Commissioner without deciding 
whether his allegation of refusal of employment is true or 
not ordred for payment of compensation of Rs. 5,000. Being 
aggrieved by the said order he preferred an appeal to the 
Hon’blc Court, while dismissing the appeal the Hon’bie Court 
observed that if the workman w not allowed to render ser¬ 
vice and is not paid wages it shall be open to him to 
approach the appropriate authority for redress. In view 
of such direction ht, raised a dispute which was admitted lo 
conciliation and the same having failed was referred to his 
Tribunal for adjudication. 

3. The first padty-management challenging the maintain¬ 
ability of the reference hay urged that the workman though 
joined service on 11-7-88 but thereafter without any reason¬ 
able excuse remained absent which amounts to abandon¬ 
ment of service and in this view of the matter he is not 
entitled to any relief in the present case. 

4. During hearing the management remained absent for 

which exparte evidence wan recorded. The workman in 
his evidence has deposed that aftei being denied of job and 
compensation due to the accident which he met in course of 
his employment, he approached from post to p.llar and 

having failed in his attempt has taken the last resort by 

raising the present dispute. In his unchallenged testimony 
he has emphatically stated that he approached the manage¬ 
ment to permit him to join his duty but the (same was 

turned-down. There having no reason to disbelieve his 

unchallenged evidence I hold that denial of employment by 
the management is illegal and unjustified. 

5. In the result, the action of the management under re¬ 
ference being not justified, the workman should be rein¬ 
stated in service with full back wages with effect from; 
1-2-83 and the back wages be paid wilhin three months 
from the date of publication of this Award.
